…rse, ast]      --reporter <REPORTER>          Style of error reporting          [possible values: tty, gcc, json, gitlab]      --stdin-filepath <filepath>          Path to use in reporting for stdin      --verbose          Enable debug logging output  -c, --config <CONFIG_PATH>          Path to the squawk config file (.squawk.toml)      --assume-in-transaction          Assume that a transaction will wrap each SQL file when run by a migration tool          Use --no-assume-in-transaction to override any config file that sets this      --no-error-on-unmatched-pattern          Do not exit with an error when provided path patterns do not match any files  -h, --help          Print help (see a summary with '-h')  -V, --version          Print version

---
name: SQL Review

on:
  pull_request:
    paths:
      - 'migrations/**'
      - '.squawk.toml'
      - '.github/workflows/sql-review.yml'

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write  # squawk-action posts inline review comments

jobs:
  sql-review:
    name: Squawk migration lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout PR head
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Lint migrations with squawk
        uses: sbdchd/squawk-action@v2
        with:
          pattern: 'migrations/*.sql'
          version: 'latest'
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
```

This catches future regressions where `.squawk.toml` silently weakens a rule.\n\n## Working directory\n\n`/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.claude/worktrees/feat+bytebase-tiered-plan-a`\n\nVerify branch BEFORE committing:\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.claude/worktrees/feat+bytebase-tiered-plan-a\ngit rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD\n```\n\nMUST output `feat/bytebase-tiered-plan-a`. Else BLOCKED.\n\n## Context\n\n`.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql` (Task 4 committed) — 3 fixtures, each violating exactly one default-on squawk rule (`ban-drop-table`, `adding-required-field`, `require-concurrent-index-creation`).\n\nThis workflow runs squawk against those fixtures and **expects failure**. If squawk exits 0 (i.e. the fixtures somehow pass), the self-test job fails loudly — meaning `.squawk.toml` has been weakened or the fixtures no longer match.\n\nBecause the squawk-action ALWAYS posts findings to the PR API and ALWAYS fails the step on findings, the cleanest way to invert this is to:\n1. Run squawk via its CLI binary directly (not via the action) so we control exit-code interpretation.\n2. Or, use the squawk-action with `continue-on-error: true` and then check `steps.<id>.outcome == 'failure'`.\n\nWe will use approach #2 (consistent with how the original spec did this) — let GitHub Actions' `continue-on-error` swallow the failure, then assert the outcome ourselves.\n\n## Project rules\n\n- Commit signed off (`-s`), no Co-Authored-By trailers.\n\n## Your task\n\n**Step 1: Create `.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml`**\n\n```yaml\nname: SQL Review Policy Self-Test\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n    paths:\n      - '.squawk.toml'\n      - '.squawk/fixtures/**'\n      - '.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml'\n  pull_request:\n    paths:\n      - '.squawk.toml'\n      - '.squawk/fixtures/**'\n      - '.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml'\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n\njobs:\n  policy-selftest:\n    name: Squawk fires on banned fixtures\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      - name: Run squawk against banned fixtures (must fail)\n        id: lint\n        continue-on-error: true\n        uses: sbdchd/squawk-action@v2\n        with:\n          pattern: '.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql'\n          version: 'latest'\n        env:\n          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n\n      - name: Assert squawk rejected the fixtures\n        if: steps.lint.outcome == 'success'\n        run: |\n          echo \"::error::Squawk did NOT reject the banned fixtures.\"\n          echo \"Either the fixtures no longer match a rule, or .squawk.toml has weakened.\"\n          echo \"Inspect .squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql and .squawk.toml.\"\n          exit 1\n\n      - name: Confirm squawk fired\n        if: steps.lint.outcome == 'failure'\n        run: echo \"Squawk correctly rejected the banned fixtures.\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Validate YAML**\n\nRun: `python3 -c 'import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open(\".github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml\"))'`\nExpected: exit code 0, no output.\n\n**Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml\ngit commit -s -m \"ci: add squawk policy self-test\n\nRuns squawk against intentionally-bad fixtures and asserts it rejects\nthem. Triggered on push to main and on PRs touching .squawk.toml,\nthe fixtures, or this workflow. Catches future edits that silently\nweaken the rule set.\"\n```\n\n**Step 4: Verify branch + commit**\n\n```bash\ngit log --oneline -1\ngit rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD\n```\n\nMUST be on `feat/bytebase-tiered-plan-a`. Else BLOCKED.\n\n## Reporting\n\n- Status\n- Branch\n- Commit SHA\n- YAML validation result"

If the user doesn't have Node, the target's failure mode is self-explanatory.\n\nThe action's `squawk-action@v2` uses the published `squawk-cli` under the hood, so behavior matches CI.\n\n`.squawk.toml` is at the repo root; squawk auto-discovers it.\n\n## Project rules\n\n- Commit signed off (`-s`), no Co-Authored-By trailers.\n\n## Your task\n\n**Step 1: Confirm Makefile uses tabs for recipes (GNU make requirement)**\n\nRun: `head -30 Makefile`\nNote the existing style (`$(CURDIR)` vs `$$PWD`, use of `##` help comments, etc.) so the new target blends in.\n\nIf the existing Makefile uses `##` comments after target names for help-target parsing, KEEP that convention. **Important — discovered while running locally:** 271 findings against existing 43 migrations. Full-tree linting would make the check unusable as a required PR gate (every migration PR would fail on pre-existing debt).

The squawk-action README's **advanced usage** pattern lints only files modified in the PR — same approach Raven already takes with golangci-lint `--new-from-rev=HEAD`. Need to retrofit Task 5 (workflow) and Task 7 (Makefile) to use diff-based selection. The self-test (Task 6) is fine — it deliberately lints a fixed fixture path.

Sending the fix back to the implementer.

{
  "to": "a267732a5a4771dca",
  "summary": "Switch to diff-based linting",
  "message": "Your run revealed 271 squawk findings against existing migrations. Making `sql-review.yml` a required check while those exist would block every future migration PR.\n\nWe need to switch the **PR-time workflow** and the **Makefile target** to lint only files modified in the PR (same pattern golangci-lint uses in this repo via `--new-from-rev`).\n\nThree small commits, in order:\n\n## Commit A — switch `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml` to diff-based\n\nReplace the file contents (currently at `55e39e82`) with:\n\n```yaml\nname: SQL Review\n\non:\n  pull_request:\n    paths:\n      - 'migrations/**'\n      - '.squawk.toml'\n      - '.github/workflows/sql-review.yml'\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n  pull-requests: write\n\njobs:\n  sql-review:\n    name: Squawk migration lint\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout full history (needed for diff vs base)\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n        with:\n          fetch-depth: 0\n\n      - name: Collect migrations modified in this PR\n        id: changed\n        run: |\n          modified=\"$(git diff --diff-filter=d --name-only \\\n            origin/${GITHUB_BASE_REF}...origin/${GITHUB_HEAD_REF} \\\n            -- 'migrations/*.sql' | tr '\\n' ' ')\"\n          echo \"Modified migrations: ${modified:-<none>}\"\n          echo \"files=${modified}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n\n      - name: Skip if no migrations modified\n        if: steps.changed.outputs.files == ''\n        run: echo \"No migrations changed in this PR — nothing to lint.\"\n\n      - name: Lint modified migrations with squawk\n        if: steps.changed.outputs.files != ''\n        uses: sbdchd/squawk-action@v2\n        with:\n          files: ${{ steps.changed.outputs.files }}\n          version: 'latest'\n        env:\n          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n```\n\nValidate YAML, commit:\n\n```\ngit add .github/workflows/sql-review.yml\ngit commit -s -m \"ci(sql-review): lint only migrations modified in the PR\n\nFull-tree squawk run flagged 271 pre-existing findings across the 43\nshipped migrations. Switching to the diff-based 'advanced usage'\npattern (per squawk-action README) so the required check enforces\nthe rule set against NEW migrations only, mirroring the lint\n--new-from-rev policy used elsewhere in the repo.\"\n```\n\n## Commit B — make Makefile target diff-based too\n\nReplace the `sql-lint-local` recipe in `Makefile` so it mirrors CI semantics. The recipe should run squawk against `migrations/*.sql` files modified versus `origin/main` (so a developer sees what CI will see for their PR).\n\n```makefile\n.PHONY: sql-lint-local\nsql-lint-local:\n\t@base=$$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main 2>/dev/null || echo HEAD~); \\\n\tfiles=$$(git diff --diff-filter=d --name-only $$base...HEAD -- 'migrations/*.sql' | tr '\\n' ' '); \\\n\tif [ -z \"$$files\" ]; then \\\n\t\techo \"No migrations modified vs origin/main — nothing to lint.\"; \\\n\telse \\\n\t\techo \"Linting modified migrations: $$files\"; \\\n\t\tnpx --yes squawk-cli@latest $$files; \\\n\tfi\n```\n\nTABS for the recipe lines, not spaces. The recipe is one long shell command joined by `\\` so the variables stay in one shell invocation.\n\nRun `make sql-lint-local`. Two expected outcomes:\n- No modified migrations on this branch → prints the \"nothing to lint\" message, exit 0.\n- Some modified migrations → squawk runs against just those files. Report exit code and finding count if any.\n\nCommit:\n\n```\ngit add Makefile\ngit commit -s -m \"build(sql-lint-local): lint only migrations modified vs origin/main\n\nMirrors the CI workflow (Commit A in this branch): full-tree linting\ntrips on 271 pre-existing findings, which is tracked separately.\nThis target now answers the question 'will my PR's migrations pass\nthe SQL review check?', not 'how clean is the whole migrations\ndirectory?'.\"\n```\n\n## Commit C — open the backlog tracking note\n\nWe'll address pre-existing findings in a follow-up. Each\nlegitimate destructive op should land an inline `-- squawk-ignore <rule>`\ncomment with rationale; other findings should be fixed via forward\nmigrations where feasible.\n```\n\nCommit:\n\n```\ngit add docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md\ngit commit -s -m \"docs(spec): record squawk backlog snapshot\n\n271 findings across 43 existing migrations under the default ruleset.\nTracked here so the post-merge follow-up has the starting numbers.\"\n```\n\n## Verification after all three commits\n\n```bash\ngit log --oneline -4\ngit rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD\n```\n\nBranch MUST be `feat/bytebase-tiered-plan-a`. 'migrations/**'\n      - '.squawk.toml'\n      - '.github/workflows/sql-review.yml'\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n  pull-requests: write\n\njobs:\n  sql-review:\n    name: Squawk migration lint\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout full history (needed for diff vs base)\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n        with:\n          fetch-depth: 0\n\n      - name: Collect migrations modified in this PR\n        id: changed\n        run: |\n          modified=\"$(git diff --diff-filter=d --name-only \\\n            origin/${GITHUB_BASE_REF}...origin/${GITHUB_HEAD_REF} \\\n            -- 'migrations/*.sql' | tr '\\n' ' ')\"\n          echo \"Modified migrations: ${modified:-<none>}\"\n          echo \"files=${modified}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n\n      - name: Skip if no migrations modified\n        if: steps.changed.outputs.files == ''\n        run: echo \"No migrations changed in this PR — nothing to lint.\"\n\n      - name: Lint modified migrations with squawk\n        if: steps.changed.outputs.files != ''\n        uses: sbdchd/squawk-action@v2\n        with:\n          files: ${{ steps.changed.outputs.files }}\n          version: 'latest'\n        env:\n          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n```\n\nValidate YAML, commit:\n\n```\ngit add .github/workflows/sql-review.yml\ngit commit -s -m \"ci(sql-review): lint only migrations modified in the PR\n\nFull-tree squawk run flagged 271 pre-existing findings across the 43\nshipped migrations. The recipe is one long shell command joined by `\\` so the variables stay in one shell invocation.\n\nRun `make sql-lint-local`. Two expected outcomes:\n- No modified migrations on this branch → prints the \"nothing to lint\" message, exit 0.\n- Some modified migrations → squawk runs against just those files. Report exit code and finding count if any.\n\nCommit:\n\n```\ngit add Makefile\ngit commit -s -m \"build(sql-lint-local): lint only migrations modified vs origin/main\n\nMirrors the CI workflow (Commit A in this branch): full-tree linting\ntrips on 271 pre-existing findings, which is tracked separately.\nThis target now answers the question 'will my PR's migrations pass\nthe SQL review check?', not 'how clean is the whole migrations\ndirectory?'.\"\n```\n\n## Commit C — open the backlog tracking note\n\nWe'll address pre-existing findings in a follow-up. Workflow file: `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml`. Config: `.squawk.toml` at repo root. Required check on `main` (Task 9 documents the one-time branch-protection step).\n- **Self-test workflow:** `.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml` runs squawk against `.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql` and asserts non-zero exit. Catches regressions in the rule set.\n- **Local lint:** `make sql-lint-local` lints only migrations modified vs `origin/main` on the current branch. Diff-based linting means new PRs don't trip on these; backlog is tracked in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md` (the design spec) for incremental cleanup.\n- **Escape hatch:** per-statement `-- squawk-ignore <rule-name>` comment immediately before the offending line. Reviewers scrutinise `squawk-ignore` lines in the PR diff. There is NO PR-label-based escape hatch (we removed that from the original Bytebase-flavoured spec because squawk doesn't support it and the inline approach is cleaner anyway — audit trail co-located with the change).\n- **Project commit rules:** every commit signed off; no Co-Authored-By trailers.\n\n## Project rules\n\n- Commit signed off (`-s`), no Co-Authored-By trailers.\n\n## Your task\n\n**Step 1: Create `docs/runbooks/migrations.md`** with the following content. You may lightly edit wording for clarity but keep the SECTION HEADERS and the COMMAND BLOCKS exactly as written.\n\n````markdown\n# Migrations Runbook\n\n## How migrations work in Raven\n\n- **Source-of-truth:** `migrations/NNNNN_<name>.sql`, applied by `pressly/goose v3`.\n- **Embedding:** the API binary embeds `migrations/` at build time (`migrations/embed.go`). Operators do not ship the SQL files separately.\n- **Apply path (today):** edge profile sets `RAVEN_DB_AUTO_MIGRATE=true` (default); `db.RunMigrations` applies pending migrations at startup.\n- **Apply path (cloud, Plan B):** `RAVEN_DB_AUTO_MIGRATE=false`; an out-of-band migrator (Bytebase server, introduced in Plan B) applies migrations through a `dev → staging → prod` pipeline with human approval at the prod stage.\n- **Startup safety check:** `db.VerifyMigrationsState` ALWAYS runs after `RunMigrations`. It compares the count of distinct currently-applied versions in `goose_db_version` (latest row per `version_id`) with the count of embedded `*.sql` files. Mismatch → `log.Fatalf` → process exits → `/readyz` stays red. This gates the cloud profile on its out-of-band migrator catching up; on edge it is belt-and-braces.\n\n## Authoring a migration\n\n1. Create `migrations/NNNNN_<descriptive_name>.sql`. `NNNNN` is the next sequential 5-digit prefix.\n2. Use the `-- +goose Up` directive at the top. **No down migrations** — rollbacks happen via a new forward migration or `pgBackRest` restore.\n3. Run the local lint before pushing:\n\n   ```bash\n   make sql-lint-local\n   ```\n\n   This runs squawk against the migrations modified on your branch versus `origin/main` — same files CI will lint on your PR.\n\n4. Open a PR. The **SQL Review** GitHub Actions check runs `squawk` against the modified migrations and posts inline review comments on findings. Findings fail the check → PR cannot merge until resolved.\n\n## The SQL review check\n\n- **Workflow:** `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml`\n- **Engine:** `sbdchd/squawk-action@v2` (server-less, downloads the `squawk` CLI binary in CI)\n- **Config:** `.squawk.toml` at repo root (`assume_in_transaction=true`, `pg_version=18.0`, default ruleset)\n- **Scope:** lints only migrations **modified in the current PR** (diff-based selection vs the PR base). Pre-existing migrations are NOT re-linted, mirroring this repo's `golangci-lint --new-from-rev=HEAD` convention.\n\nThe pre-existing backlog (271 findings across the 43 shipped migrations under squawk's default ruleset) is tracked in the design spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md`. It will be paid down incrementally — do NOT bundle backlog cleanup into unrelated PRs.\n\n### Escape hatch — intentional rule violation\n\nSome legitimate migrations need an operation squawk would normally flag (e.g. dropping a column after a multi-PR deprecation, or doing a small `CREATE INDEX` synchronously on a table guaranteed to be empty at apply time).\n\nPer-statement override is an inline comment immediately before the offending SQL:\n\n```sql\n-- +goose Up\n-- Removes the legacy zitadel_users mirror table; superseded by SuperTokens\n-- session storage in migration 00037. Confirmed empty in prod via Bytebase\n-- before this PR; see ADR-2026-05-12 for the full sequence.\n-- squawk-ignore ban-drop-table\nDROP TABLE zitadel_users;\n```\n\n- The `-- squawk-ignore <rule-name>` comment skips that one rule for the next statement.\n- Multiple rules in one comment: `-- squawk-ignore ban-drop-column, renaming-column`.\n- File-wide skip is supported (`-- squawk-ignore-file <rule>`) but use sparingly — it removes a guard rail for everything in that file.\n- **Reviewer responsibility:** every `squawk-ignore` line in a PR diff requires explicit reviewer attention. Look at the comment immediately above for the rationale. If it's not justified, reject the PR.\n\n### Policy self-test\n\n`.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml` runs squawk against `.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql` and asserts the rules fire. If a future PR weakens `.squawk.toml` (e.g. adds a default-on rule to `excluded_rules`), the self-test fails — catching the regression at PR time.\n\nThe fixtures intentionally violate `ban-drop-table`, `adding-required-field`, and `require-concurrent-index-creation`. They are NOT real migrations and are not under the `migrations/` directory.\n\n## Recovery from a failed migration\n\n### Edge profile\n\nIf `RunMigrations` fails:\n\n1. The `cmd/api` process exits non-zero. The operator sees the goose error in the API logs.\n2. Restore the database from the last pre-migration `pgBackRest` base backup + WAL replay up to the LSN immediately before the failed migration's `BEGIN`. Detailed procedure: `docs/runbooks/demo-restore.md`.\n3. Fix the migration in a new PR.\n4. Re-deploy.\n\nIf `RunMigrations` succeeds but `VerifyMigrationsState` fails:\n\n- This indicates an embedded-file count vs `goose_db_version` mismatch. Most likely cause: a partial restore from backup that left `goose_db_version` out of sync.\n- Inspect `goose_db_version` (`SELECT version_id, is_applied, tstamp FROM goose_db_version ORDER BY id`).\n- Run `goose status` against the live DB using the on-disk migrations directory (not the embed) for a side-by-side view.\n- Resolve by either re-applying missed migrations through `goose up` or restoring further back via `pgBackRest`.\n\n### Cloud profile (planned, Plan B)\n\nBytebase marks the migration Issue `FAILED` and halts the dev/staging/prod pipeline. The DBA inspects the SQL in the Bytebase UI, fixes it (either via a hotfix issue in Bytebase or by raising a forward-fix PR), and retries the stage. The affected tenant's `cmd/api` pods stay on the old version because `/readyz` stays red while `VerifyMigrationsState` fails. Other tenants are unaffected.\n\n## Required CI checks (branch protection)\n\nThe following job must be a required status check on `main`:\n\n- `Squawk migration lint` (the `sql-review` job in `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml`)\n\nThis is configured manually under **Settings → Branches → Branch protection rules → main → Require status checks to pass before merging**. Add the check name above. The check is registered with GitHub only after the workflow has run at least once.\n\n## References\n\n- Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md`\n- Implementation plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-plan-a.md`\n- Squawk rules reference: https://squawkhq.com/docs/rules\n- Squawk CLI: https://squawkhq.com/docs/cli\n- pgBackRest restore: `docs/runbooks/demo-restore.md`\n````\n\n**Step 2: Commit**\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p docs/runbooks\ngit add docs/runbooks/migrations.md\ngit commit -s -m \"docs(runbook): add migrations runbook\n\nDeveloper-facing SOP for migrations: authoring rules, the squawk PR\ncheck, the squawk-ignore escape hatch, recovery from failed applies\non edge, and the cloud-profile recovery path planned for Plan B.\nCalls out the 271-finding backlog and the diff-based linting policy\nthat protects new PRs from it.\"\n```\n\n**Step 3: Verify**\n\n```bash\ngit log --oneline -1\ngit rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD\n```\n\nBranch MUST be `feat/bytebase-tiered-plan-a`. Else BLOCKED.\n\n## Reporting\n\n- Status\n- Branch\n- Commit SHA\n- File path created\n- Any clarifications you made to the runbook content beyond what the prompt provided (and why)"

68	
69	## PR-time SQL Review (Layer 1)
70	
71	**Tool:** `bytebase/sql-review-action` (official GitHub Action, no server required).
72	
73	**Workflow:** new job `sql-review` in `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml`. Triggers on PRs that touch `migrations/**`. Wired into branch protection on `main` as a required check.
74	
75	**Policy file:** `.bytebase/sql-review.json`. Initial ruleset:
76	
77	| Rule | Severity | Reason |
78	|---|---|---|
79	| `naming.column` (snake_case) | ERROR | Consistency with 43 existing migrations |
80	| `column.required` (created_at, updated_at on new tables) | WARNING | Matches existing convention |
81	| `column.no-null` on PK / FK columns | ERROR | Catch obvious mistakes |
82	| `statement.disallow-commit` | ERROR | Migrations must be transactional |
83	| `statement.disallow-truncate` | ERROR | Destructive ops need explicit override |
84	| `statement.disallow-rm-tbl-cascade` | ERROR | Destructive ops need explicit override |
85	| `statement.add-column-without-default` (non-null) | WARNING | Forces backfill thinking |
86	| `statement.create-index-concurrently` | WARNING | Edge OK, cloud tenants may be large |
87	| `index.no-duplicate` | ERROR | Catch redundant indexes |
88	| `schema.backward-compatibility` | WARNING | Heads-up on breaking renames/drops |
89	
90	**Failure UX:** action posts inline review comments on offending file/line. ERROR blocks merge; WARNING surfaces as a reviewer checklist.
91	
92	**Escape hatch:** PR label `migration:approved-destructive` skips destructive-op rules. Label applicable only by maintainers (codeowner enforcement). Documented in the migration SOP.
93	
94	## GitOps Wiring + History Coexistence (Cloud, Layer 2)
95	
96	Bytebase **pulls** from GitHub.
97	
98	1. Cloud-mode Bytebase server has a project `raven` with a VCS provider bound to `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven`, branch `main`, base path `migrations/`.
99	2. On every push to `main` touching `migrations/**`, Bytebase's webhook receiver enumerates new files (goose numeric prefix doubles as a valid Bytebase migration version) and opens an Issue of type "Database Schema Update" per file.
100	3. The Issue runs through a pipeline with three stages — `dev → staging → prod` — each applying to its tenant DB pool, with per-stage approval policies:
101	   - `dev`: auto-apply
102	   - `staging`: auto-apply after dev success
103	   - `prod`: requires one human approver (Bytebase role: `DBA` or `Owner`)
104	4. SQL Review runs again on each stage (defense in depth) using the same policy file as PR-time.
105	
106	### History-table coexistence
107	
108	Bytebase by default writes to its own `bytebase` schema per managed DB. We override this:
109	
110	- Each Bytebase database connection is registered with a migration-history hint pointing at `public.goose_db_version`.
111	- When Bytebase applies migration `00044_foo.sql`, it writes `(version_id=44, is_applied=true, tstamp=now())` — same row shape goose writes.
112	- When goose applies the same file on an edge node, it writes the same row.
113	- A migration is "applied" iff its row exists with `is_applied=true`. Both systems agree.
114	
115	**Trade-off accepted:** Bytebase's own schema-comparison features (its `bytebase` metadata schema) won't exist in tenant DBs. We choose shared state with goose over those.
116	
117	### Drift detection
118	
119	- Bytebase's drift-detection job runs hourly per tenant DB. Diffs live schema against expected schema derived from applied `migrations/*.sql`. Opens a `drift`-tagged Issue on mismatch.
120	- Edge profile adds a startup check `db.VerifyMigrationsState(ctx)` (see next section) — cheap row-count assertion against `goose_db_version`.
121	
122	## Cloud-mode Config + `cmd/api` Changes
123	
124	**Config:** reuse existing `cfg.Database.AutoMigrate`. No new field.
125	
126	| Profile | `AutoMigrate` | Source |
127	|---|---|---|
128	| Edge (default) | `true` | Hardcoded default in `config.Defaults()` |
129	| Cloud | `false` | `RAVEN_DB_AUTO_MIGRATE=false` in cloud Helm/compose values |
130	
131	**`cmd/api/main.go` changes:**
132	
133	```go
134	// existing
135	if cfg.Database.AutoMigrate {
136	    if err := db.RunMigrations(ctx, cfg.Database.URL); err != nil {
137	        return fmt.Errorf("run migrations: %w", err)
138	    }
139	}
140	
141	// NEW — always run, both profiles
142	if err := db.VerifyMigrationsState(ctx, cfg.Database.URL); err != nil {
143	    return fmt.Errorf("verify migrations: %w", err)
144	}
145	```
146	
147	`VerifyMigrationsState` lives next to `RunMigrations` in `internal/db/migrate.go`. It compares `count(* from goose_db_version where is_applied=true)` to `len(migrations.FS files)`. Mismatch → return error → `cmd/api` exits or readiness probe stays red.
148	
149	**Readiness:** `/readyz` depends on `VerifyMigrationsState` passing. `/healthz` (liveness) does not — we don't want K8s killing pods during a temporary mismatch.
150	
151	**Cloud deploy ordering (Kubernetes / ArgoCD):**
152	
153	1. CI merges PR to `main`.
154	2. Bytebase webhook fires → creates Issue → pipeline starts.
155	3. Bytebase applies migration to staging tenants (auto).
156	4. Human approves prod stage in Bytebase UI.
157	5. Bytebase applies to prod tenants → writes `goose_db_version` row.
158	6. Bytebase "applied" event triggers ArgoCD/Flux sync of `cmd/api`.
159	7. `cmd/api` pods roll out.
160	8. Each pod runs `VerifyMigrationsState` → passes → ready.
161	
162	If step 7 fires before step 5 completes for a tenant: `VerifyMigrationsState` fails on the affected pod, readiness stays red, traffic doesn't shift, K8s holds at old version. Self-healing.
```

ERROR blocks merge; WARNING surfaces as a reviewer checklist.\n\n**Escape hatch:** PR label `migration:approved-destructive` skips destructive-op rules. Label applicable only by maintainers (codeowner enforcement). Documented in the migration SOP.",
  "new_string": "## PR-time SQL Review (Layer 1)\n\n> **Pivot note (2026-05-25):** the original design proposed `bytebase/sql-review-action` as a server-less PR-time linter. During implementation we confirmed no such standalone action exists — Bytebase's `bytebase-action check` requires a running Bytebase server (`--url`, `--service-account`). Plan A pivoted to [`sbdchd/squawk-action@v2`](https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk-action), a Postgres-specific server-less linter. The Plan A goals (catch unsafe DDL before merge, no server runtime, edge-footprint-friendly) are unchanged; only the tool differs. Plan B still introduces a Bytebase server in the cloud control plane for workflow/approval features.\n\n**Tool:** [`sbdchd/squawk-action@v2`](https://github.com/sbdchd/squawk-action) — wraps the [`squawk`](https://squawkhq.com) CLI, no server required.\n\n**Workflow:** `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml`. Triggers on PRs that touch `migrations/**`, `.squawk.toml`, or the workflow file itself. Wired into branch protection on `main` as a required check (manual step — documented in `docs/runbooks/migrations.md`).\n\n**Scope:** lints only migrations **modified in the current PR** (diff-based selection via `git diff origin/$BASE...origin/$HEAD`). The initial squawk run against the 43 shipped migrations surfaced 271 findings under the default ruleset, which is pre-existing debt — diff-based linting protects new PRs from it while a separate backlog initiative pays it down. Mirrors the `golangci-lint --new-from-rev=HEAD` convention used elsewhere in the repo.\n\n**Config file:** `.squawk.toml` at the repo root. Squawk auto-discovers it.\n\n```toml\nassume_in_transaction = true   # goose wraps each migration in BEGIN/COMMIT\npg_version = \"18.0\"            # matches Raven runtime; gates version-specific rules\nexcluded_rules = []            # full default ruleset on; per-statement exceptions go inline\n```\n\n**Initial ruleset:** squawk's default-on set (~30 rules), including `ban-drop-table`, `ban-drop-column`, `ban-truncate-cascade`, `require-concurrent-index-creation`, `require-concurrent-index-deletion`, `adding-required-field`, `prefer-text-field`, `prefer-bigint-over-int`, `require-timeout-settings`, `transaction-nesting`, `renaming-column`, `renaming-table`, `changing-column-type`, and more. Full list: <https://squawkhq.com/docs/rules>. Customisation lives in `.squawk.toml` (`excluded_rules`/`included_rules`).\n\n**Failure UX:** the action posts findings as inline review comments on the PR. Any finding fails the job → PR cannot merge until resolved.\n\n**Escape hatch:** per-statement `-- squawk-ignore <rule-name>` comment immediately before the offending SQL line. The audit trail co-locates with the change — every `squawk-ignore` in a PR diff requires explicit reviewer attention. This replaces the original PR-label-based escape hatch (squawk has no concept of labels and the inline form is cleaner). See `docs/runbooks/migrations.md` for the SOP.\n\n**Policy self-test:** `.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml` runs squawk against `.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql` (three fixtures violating `ban-drop-table`, `adding-required-field`, `require-concurrent-index-creation`) and asserts non-zero exit. Catches regressions where a future PR weakens `.squawk.toml`.\n\n**Local equivalent:** `make sql-lint-local` runs squawk via `npx squawk-cli@latest` against migrations modified versus `origin/main` — same semantics as CI."
}

195	
196	**Known compliance gap:** edge deployments lack per-stage human approval (single-operator boxes can't ask themselves). Documented in the threat model. Cloud SaaS is the profile that needs the controls.
197	
198	## Testing Strategy
199	
200	**Layer 1 — PR SQL Review action.**
201	
202	- Self-test: fixture PR in CI with an intentionally banned pattern must be rejected. `.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml`, runs on push to `main`.
203	- Policy file `.bytebase/sql-review.json` linted in CI with `bb sql-review --check-policy`.
204	
205	**Layer 2 — Migration unit tests.**
206	
207	- Existing `migrations/migrations_test.go` validates goose can parse and apply every file against a throwaway Postgres (testcontainers). Keep as-is.
208	- **Add:** test that runs all migrations then `db.VerifyMigrationsState` — asserts pass. Plus a negative test (delete a row → expect failure).
209	
210	**Layer 3 — Integration test for Bytebase ingest (cloud only).**
211	
212	- New `internal/integration/bytebase_test.go` (gated by `//go:build integration`):
213	  1. Spin up Postgres + Bytebase via testcontainers.
214	  2. Configure project against a local git fixture with 2 migration files.
215	  3. Trigger sync; assert Bytebase opens an Issue per file.
216	  4. Approve programmatically via Bytebase API.
217	  5. Assert files applied to target Postgres AND `goose_db_version` rows present.
218	- Runs in CI on `migrations/**` or `internal/db/**` changes only.
219	
220	**Layer 4 — Drift detection test.**
221	
222	- Apply all migrations.
223	- Manually `ALTER TABLE` out-of-band.
224	- Trigger Bytebase drift scan via API.
225	- Assert `drift`-tagged Issue is opened.
226	
227	**Out of scope:**
228	
229	- Bytebase server's own correctness (third-party; pin a known-good version).
230	- GitHub webhook path end-to-end (would need real GitHub; test the receiver-side by feeding migration files directly).
231	- New tests for edge migration path — `migrations_test.go` already covers it.
232	
233	**Local dev:** `make sql-review-local` runs the action against current branch's `migrations/**` diff so authors get the same feedback they'd get from CI before pushing.
234	
235	## What Changes vs What Stays
236	
237	| Area | Change? |
238	|---|---|
239	| `migrations/*.sql` | No change. Goose numbering preserved. |
240	| `migrations/embed.go` | No change. |
241	| `internal/db/migrate.go` | Add `VerifyMigrationsState(ctx, url) error`. |
242	| `cmd/api/main.go` | Add unconditional call to `VerifyMigrationsState` after the existing `AutoMigrate` block. |
243	| `internal/config/config.go` | No change (reuse `Database.AutoMigrate`). |
244	| `.github/workflows/` | New `sql-review.yml`, `sql-review-selftest.yml`. |
245	| `.bytebase/sql-review.json` | New policy file. |
246	| `Makefile` | New `sql-review-local` target. |
247	| Edge compose | No change. |
248	| Cloud Helm/compose | New `bytebase` service in control plane; `RAVEN_DB_AUTO_MIGRATE=false` on `cmd/api`. |
249	| Branch protection on `main` | Add `sql-review` as required check. |
250	| Migration SOP doc | Update with escape-hatch label + Bytebase approval flow. |
251	
252	## Open Questions / Plan-Time Decisions
253	
254	- Exact Bytebase version to pin (latest stable at plan time).
255	- Helm chart vs raw compose for cloud Bytebase deployment — depends on the cloud profile's overall orchestration choice, which is outside this spec.
256	- Whether to expose Bytebase UI behind the Raven control-plane SSO (SuperTokens) or as a separate auth domain initially. Defer to the cloud-profile rollout plan.
257	
258	## References
259	
260	- `internal/db/migrate.go` — current goose runner
261	- `migrations/` — 43 SQL files (00001–00043)
262	- `migrations/embed.go`, `migrations/migrations_test.go`
263	- Bytebase docs: <https://www.bytebase.com/docs/>
264	- Bytebase SQL Review GH Action: <https://github.com/bytebase/sql-review-action>

1	# Bytebase Tiered Adoption — Plan A: PR-time SQL Review + Edge Readiness
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Land the ship-anywhere half of the Bytebase tiered adoption — PR-time SQL Review action against `migrations/*.sql`, plus a `VerifyMigrationsState` startup check that gates `cmd/api` readiness. No Bytebase server runtime is introduced. This delivers value to both edge and cloud profiles today.
6	
7	**Architecture:** Two coordinated changes. (1) A new Go function `db.VerifyMigrationsState` compares `count(goose_db_version where is_applied=true and version_id>0)` against the count of embedded `*.sql` files; called unconditionally from `cmd/api/main.go` after the existing `RunMigrations` block. (2) A new GitHub Actions workflow runs `bytebase/sql-review-action` against PR diffs touching `migrations/**`, using a policy at `.bytebase/sql-review.json`. A self-test workflow exercises the policy against intentionally-bad fixtures so regressions in the rules surface immediately.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** Go 1.x, `github.com/pressly/goose/v3`, `github.com/lib/pq`, `github.com/testcontainers/testcontainers-go`, `github.com/stretchr/testify/require`, GitHub Actions, `bytebase/sql-review-action` (v1), JSON policy file.
10	
11	**Source spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md`
12	
13	**Out of scope for Plan A (deferred to Plan B):** Bytebase server deployment, VCS provider config, dev→staging→prod pipeline, drift detection, Layer 3 & 4 integration tests, history-table override on cloud DBs, SSO integration. Plan B will be written when cloud-profile orchestration choices (Helm vs compose, SSO domain) are settled.
14	
15	---
```

  "new_string": "# Bytebase Tiered Adoption — Plan A: PR-time SQL Review + Edge Readiness\n\n> **POST-EXECUTION PIVOT NOTE (2026-05-25):** Tasks 3–7 below were written assuming `bytebase/sql-review-action` existed as a server-less PR linter. The high-level goal and `VerifyMigrationsState`/cmd-api wiring (Tasks 1 & 2) are unchanged. Config: `.squawk.toml` (TOML, not JSON). (1) A new Go function `db.VerifyMigrationsState` compares the count of distinct currently-applied migration versions in `goose_db_version` (latest row per `version_id`) against the count of embedded `*.sql` files; called unconditionally from `cmd/api/main.go` after the existing `RunMigrations` block. (2) `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml` runs `sbdchd/squawk-action@v2` against PR-modified migration files only (diff-based) using `.squawk.toml` config. {
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"feat: tiered DB devops — VerifyMigrationsState + squawk PR review (Plan A)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nPlan A of the [tiered DB-devops design](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md). Two coordinated changes:\n\n1. **Edge-safety startup gate (`db.VerifyMigrationsState`):** counts the latest-state-per-version_id rows in `goose_db_version` against the embedded `*.sql` file count. Mismatch → `log.Fatalf` → `/readyz` stays red. Edge: belt-and-braces after goose. Cloud (Plan B): primary gate against API pods coming up before the out-of-band migrator finishes.\n\n2. **PR-time SQL review with `sbdchd/squawk-action@v2`:** server-less Postgres-specific linter against migrations modified in the current PR (diff-based, like `golangci-lint --new-from-rev=HEAD`). Self-test workflow asserts the rules fire against banned-pattern fixtures.\n\n### Pivot vs original spec\n\nThe original spec proposed `bytebase/sql-review-action` as a server-less PR linter. **That action doesn't exist** — Bytebase's own `bytebase-action check` requires a running Bytebase server (`--url`, `--service-account`). Plan A pivoted to squawk mid-execution. The spec + plan docs have been updated to reflect the as-shipped state, with a clear pivot note. Plan B (Bytebase server in cloud control plane) is still the design for workflow/approval features.\n\n## Files\n\n- `internal/db/migrate.go` — new `VerifyMigrationsState` + `countEmbeddedMigrations` helper\n- `internal/db/migrate_test.go` — 3 tests (happy path, mismatch, rollback+re-apply)\n- `cmd/api/main.go` — unconditional `VerifyMigrationsState` call after the existing `AutoMigrate` block\n- `.squawk.toml` — squawk config (`assume_in_transaction=true`, `pg_version=18.0`, full default ruleset)\n- `.squawk/fixtures/bad/*.sql` — 3 fixtures violating `ban-drop-table`, `adding-required-field`, `require-concurrent-index-creation`\n- `.github/workflows/sql-review.yml` — PR-time squawk run, diff-based\n- `.github/workflows/sql-review-selftest.yml` — asserts squawk rejects the fixtures\n- `Makefile` — new `sql-lint-local` target (runs squawk via `npx` against modified migrations)\n- `docs/runbooks/migrations.md` — developer SOP: authoring, escape hatch, recovery\n- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-design.md` — design spec + pivot note + backlog snapshot\n- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-23-bytebase-tiered-plan-a.md` — implementation plan + pivot note\n\n## Squawk backlog\n\nThe initial full-tree squawk run against the 43 shipped migrations surfaced **271 findings** under the default ruleset. These are pre-existing and **not blocked by this PR** — the workflow only lints PR-modified files. Counts by rule are recorded in the spec (\"Squawk Backlog Snapshot\"). A separate initiative will pay this down incrementally.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `go build ./...` clean\n- [x] `go test ./internal/db/...` — 3 new tests pass (PASS in 3.6s)\n- [x] `golangci-lint run --new-from-rev=origin/main ./...` — 0 new issues\n- [x] `make sql-lint-local` on this branch — \"nothing to lint\" (no migrations modified)\n- [x] Diff vs `origin/main` is exactly the 13 files I touched (rebased on latest main)\n- [ ] CI green on this PR\n- [ ] **Manual post-merge step:** add `Squawk migration lint` as a required status check on `main` under Settings → Branches → Branch protection rules. The check name registers with GitHub only after the workflow runs at least once (which happens on this PR). See `docs/runbooks/migrations.md` for full instructions.\n\n## Deferred to Plan B\n\nCloud-profile Bytebase server, `dev → staging → prod` approval pipeline, drift detection, history-table override on tenant DBs, SSO integration. ```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/.claude/projects/-Users-jobinlawrance-Project-raven/memory/feedback_verify_third_party_tools.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-verify-third-party-tools\ndescription: When proposing a third-party CLI/action/library as the load-bearing piece of a design, verify it actually exists and behaves as claimed before locking the spec — fetch the README via ctx_fetch_and_index, don't rely on training-data knowledge of action names\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nWhen a design proposal hinges on a specific external tool (a GitHub Action, a CLI command, a Docker image), verify the tool actually exists and matches the claimed behaviour BEFORE writing the spec around it. Don't rely on training-data recall of action names or capabilities — those are often slightly wrong or named differently than you remember.\n\n**Why:** During Plan A of the Bytebase tiered adoption, I proposed `bytebase/sql-review-action` as a server-less PR-time linter. It does not exist as a standalone action — Bytebase's own `bytebase-action check` requires a running Bytebase server. The whole spec was built on this false premise. Discovery happened mid-execution (Task 7), forcing a reset of 5 commits, a pivot to `sbdchd/squawk-action@v2`, and re-execution of Tasks 3–7. ~2 hours of subagent time wasted. The user surfaced the alternatives I should have pre-vetted.\n\n**How to apply:** Before finalising a design that names a specific external action/CLI/image:\n1. Fetch the canonical README via `ctx_fetch_and_index` (GitHub repo URL, marketplace listing, or docs page).\n2. Confirm the entry-point name, required vs optional inputs, and any server/auth dependencies.\n3. If the proposal claims \"no server needed\" or \"server-less\", confirm that explicitly — many lint tools that look standalone actually require a hosted backend.\n4. Re-do this check when the spec is approved and again at the start of plan execution — the second pass catches \"the tool was renamed last month\" cases that a single check misses.\n\nCounter-cases where this is overkill: well-established standard-library or framework tools (e.g. `actions/checkout`, `pytest`, `go test`), or tools the user has already named explicitly and asked to use. The risk is highest when *I'm* proposing the tool to fit a design shape I'm advocating for.\n"
}

## Summary

**PR #679 open, auto-merge (squash) queued:** https://github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pull/679

**What landed (Plan A):**
- `db.VerifyMigrationsState` startup gate (latest-row-per-version_id semantics — handles append-only `goose_db_version` correctly on rollback+re-apply) + 3 tests
- `cmd/api/main.go` calls it unconditionally — fatal on mismatch
- `sbdchd/squawk-action@v2` PR-time review, diff-based (mirrors `--new-from-rev`), with `.squawk.toml` + 3 banned-pattern fixtures + self-test workflow
- `make sql-lint-local` (via `npx squawk-cli`)
- `docs/runbooks/migrations.md` — authoring SOP + `-- squawk-ignore` escape hatch + recovery
- Spec + plan docs updated with pivot note explaining the Bytebase-action → squawk swap

**Mid-execution discovery:** `bytebase/sql-review-action` doesn't exist. Pivoted to squawk after surfacing the alternatives to you. Saved a feedback memory so I verify third-party tools exist before locking specs around them next time.

**271 pre-existing squawk findings** on the 43 shipped migrations are recorded in the spec as backlog — diff-based linting means new PRs aren't blocked by them.

**Manual post-merge step** (in PR description): add `Squawk migration lint` as a required check on `main` once it appears in the branch-protection picker.

**Deferred to Plan B:** Bytebase server in cloud control plane, approval pipeline, drift detection, history-table override. Write when cloud orchestration choices are settled.
